A grown-up-sized long-sleeved, ankle-length, button-front union-suit style onesie, with no hood, just a neckline and collar, with built-in gloves at the sleeve ends, and footless with elasticated ankle cuffs, in soft cotton jersey, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in an all-over sublimation print that covers the entire shirt, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of a mummy. The whole suit is the outfit, front, back, arms and legs (and any hood, gloves or feet) are printed as the matching parts of it, with the seams and the zip hidden in the pattern. The print shows wrapped bandages, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. There is no separate background, the print is cut to the onesie's shape. The print is applied to the suit's own pattern pieces, front, back, each sleeve and each leg are fully covered in the matching cloth of the outfit, with no leftover background colour between them. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided suit shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The suit is solid and opaque and nothing is real but the jersey, textures and sheens are printed pictures only. It is not a drawing of a person standing in the middle of the suit, no body, no head, no limbs and no figure are pictured, only the outfit's cloth, trims and fastenings spread over the whole suit. The outfit has bandages on the forearm and wrist, so the onesie's sleeves run to the wrist and the printed bandages sit at the sleeve ends, with nothing beyond the cuffs. Printed shadows and fold lines suggest the garments, but the suit itself is smooth, flat and uniform in material. The suit is the whole mummy outfit, not just a top, wrapped bandages. It is shown in full, from the neckline down to the ankle cuffs. Sewn-on novelty extras, kept to the edges so the print stays unbroken, with a fleece-lined hood interior in a bright colour, a fluffy pom-pom at the tip of the hood and glittery elastic cuffs. It is a bright, playful novelty onesie that is fun to touch.

Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the suit is outfit cloth. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. Everything is flat ink on opaque stretch jersey, lace, fur, metal, leather and sequins are printed patterns, never real raised or see-through material. The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth.
